```xml
  </ItemGroup>

  <ItemGroup>
    <Content Include="File.ico" />
  </ItemGroup>

  <Import Project="$(MSBuildBinPath)\Microsoft.CSharp.targets" />

  <PropertyGroup>
    <BeforeBuildDependsOn>$(BeforeBuildDependsOn);LogRustPaths</BeforeBuildDependsOn>
    <BuildDependsOn>$(BuildDependsOn);BuildRustProject</BuildDependsOn>
    <AfterBuildDependsOn>$(AfterBuildDependsOn);RunBuildCopyTool</AfterBuildDependsOn>
    <CleanDependsOn>$(CleanDependsOn);CleanRustFiles</CleanDependsOn>
  </PropertyGroup>

  <Target Name="LogRustPaths">
    <Message Text="=== HandlerGui LogRustPaths ===" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Configuration: $(Configuration)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Platform: $(Platform)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="ProjectDir: $(ProjectDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RustProjectRoot: $(RustProjectRoot)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RustDllSourcePath: $(RustDllSourcePath)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RustRuntimeDir: $(RustRuntimeDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="BuildCopyToolExe: $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="TargetDir: $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="BuildRustProject" Condition="'$(BuildRust)' == 'true'">
    <Message Text="Building Rust project in $(RustProjectRoot)" Importance="high" />
    <Exec Command="cargo +rust9x dll-debug" WorkingDirectory="$(RustProjectRoot)" Condition=" '$(Configuration)' == 'Debug' " />
    <Exec Command="cargo +rust9x dll-release" WorkingDirectory="$(RustProjectRoot)" Condition=" '$(Configuration)' == 'Release' " />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool">
    <Message Text="=== RunBuildCopyTool ===" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Tool: $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="DLL : $(RustDllSourcePath)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RT  : $(RustRuntimeDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="OUT : $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />

    <Error Text="BuildCopyTool not found: $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Condition="!Exists('$(BuildCopyToolExe)')" />
    <Error Text="Rust DLL not found: $(RustDllSourcePath)" Condition="!Exists('$(RustDllSourcePath)')" />

    <Exec Command="&quot;$(BuildCopyToolExe)&quot; &quot;$(RustDllSourcePath)&quot; &quot;$(RustRuntimeDir)&quot; &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;" />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="CleanRustFiles">
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)')" />
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)msvcr80.dll" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcr80.dll')" />
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)msvcp80.dll" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcp80.dll')" />
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)msvcm80.dll" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcm80.dll')" />
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT\*.*" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT')" />
    <RemoveDir Directories="$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT')" />
    <Message Text="Rust artifacts cleaned." Importance="high" />
  </Target>
</Project>
```

This changes the project to:

- stop doing direct MSBuild copy tasks,
- call `BuildCopyTool.exe` after build,
- log the exact DLL/runtime/output paths,
- fail clearly if the tool or DLL is missing.

One thing to verify next is that `BuildCopyTool.exe` is actually being built to the path this project expects.

**ChatGPT** *(05.08.2026, 18:05:57)*

That's because **MSBuild does not automatically execute another project just because it exists in the solution.** Your `BuildCopyTool` compiled, but nothing ever invokes `BuildCopyTool.exe`.

There are basically three ways to do this.

### Option 1 (recommended): Run BuildCopyTool from the projects

In both `Rust9xWindowsAuth.csproj` and `HandlerGui.csproj` add:

```xml
<PropertyGroup>
  <BuildCopyToolExe>$(SolutionDir)BuildCopyTool\bin\$(Configuration)\BuildCopyTool.exe</BuildCopyToolExe>
</PropertyGroup>

<Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool" AfterTargets="Build">

  <Message Text="Running BuildCopyTool..." Importance="high"/>

  <Exec
      Command="&quot;$(BuildCopyToolExe)&quot;
      &quot;$(MSBuildProjectDirectory)&quot;
      &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;
      &quot;$(Configuration)&quot;"
      Condition="Exists('$(BuildCopyToolExe)')" />

</Target>
```

Your BuildCopyTool then decides whether it's copying Rust files for `Rust9xWindowsAuth` or `HandlerGui`.

---

### Option 2 (better): Only run from HandlerGui

Since HandlerGui already references Rust9xWindowsAuth, only the final executable actually needs the Rust DLL.

Then only HandlerGui contains

```xml
<Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool" AfterTargets="Build">

    <Exec
        Command="&quot;$(SolutionDir)BuildCopyTool\bin\$(Configuration)\BuildCopyTool.exe&quot;
        &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;
        &quot;$(Configuration)&quot;" />

</Target>
```

Rust9xWindowsAuth contains **no copy logic whatsoever.**

This avoids duplicate copying.

---

### Option 3 (best overall): BuildCopyTool as an MSBuild Task

Instead of launching an EXE, BuildCopyTool becomes an MSBuild Task DLL.

Then your project simply says

```xml
<UsingTask
    TaskName="BuildCopyTool.CopyRustFiles"
    AssemblyFile="$(SolutionDir)BuildCopyTool\bin\$(Configuration)\BuildCopyTool.dll"/>

<Target Name="AfterBuild">
    <CopyRustFiles
        Configuration="$(Configuration)"
        TargetDir="$(TargetDir)"
        ProjectDir="$(ProjectDir)"/>
</Target>
```

No process creation, no console window, proper MSBuild logging, build errors appear naturally.

---

## Why yours didn't execute

Your rebuild log ends with

```
BuildCopyTool -> ...\BuildCopyTool.exe
```

and **nothing afterwards**.

If it had executed, you'd see something like

```
Running BuildCopyTool...
BuildCopyTool.exe ...
Copying rust9x_windows_auth.dll
Copied Microsoft.VC80.CRT
Finished BuildCopyTool.
```

The absence of those lines means there is **no `<Exec>` target anywhere** that launches the executable.

---

I would actually simplify the entire solution to this:

```
BuildCopyTool
    ↓
Rust9xWindowsAuth
    ↓
HandlerGui
```

with **only `HandlerGui`** executing `BuildCopyTool` after a successful build. Since `HandlerGui` is the final application, that's the only place where the native DLL and CRT need to be copied. It also guarantees the copy runs exactly once during a normal solution build.

---

    <Message Text="TargetDir: $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool">
    <Message Text="=== RunBuildCopyTool ===" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Tool: $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="DLL : $(RustDllSourcePath)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RT  : $(RustRuntimeDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="OUT : $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />

    <Error Text="Rust DLL not found: $(RustDllSourcePath)" Condition="!Exists('$(RustDllSourcePath)')" />

    <Exec Command="&quot;$(BuildCopyToolExe)&quot; &quot;$(RustDllSourcePath)&quot; &quot;$(RustRuntimeDir)&quot; &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;" />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="CleanRustFiles">
    <Delete Files="$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)" Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)')" />

The important part is this line:

```xml
<BuildDependsOn>$(BuildDependsOn);LogRustPaths;BuildBuildCopyTool;RunBuildCopyTool</BuildDependsOn>
```

That makes the copy tool part of the actual build chain, instead of relying on `AfterBuildDependsOn`, which was the part not firing for you.

If you want, I can also patch `BuildCopyTool.cs` so it accepts the exact two Rust paths and logs a full directory listing when the DLL is missing.

**You** *(05.08.2026, 18:09:30)*

and make using System;
using System.IO;

namespace BuildCopyTool
{
    internal static class Program
    {
        private static int Main(string[] args)
        {
            try
            {
                Console.WriteLine("=== BuildCopyTool ===");

                if (args.Length < 3)
                {
                    PrintUsage();
                    return 2;
                }

                string sourceFile = args[0];
                string sourceRuntimeDir = args[1];
                string destinationDir = args[2];

                sourceFile = NormalizePath(sourceFile);
                sourceRuntimeDir = NormalizePath(sourceRuntimeDir);
                destinationDir = NormalizePath(destinationDir);

                Console.WriteLine("Source DLL      : " + sourceFile);
                Console.WriteLine("Source runtime  : " + sourceRuntimeDir);
                Console.WriteLine("Destination dir : " + destinationDir);
                Console.WriteLine();

                DumpDirectory("SOURCE DLL PARENT", Path.GetDirectoryName(sourceFile));
                DumpDirectory("SOURCE RUNTIME", sourceRuntimeDir);

                if (!File.Exists(sourceFile))
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("ERROR: DLL not found: " + sourceFile);
                    return 3;
                }

                if (!Directory.Exists(destinationDir))
                {
                    Directory.CreateDirectory(destinationDir);
                    Console.WriteLine("Created destination directory.");
                }

                string dllDest = Path.Combine(destinationDir, Path.GetFileName(sourceFile));
                CopyFile(sourceFile, dllDest);

                CopyRuntimeFiles(sourceRuntimeDir, destinationDir);

                Console.WriteLine();
                Console.WriteLine("BuildCopyTool completed successfully.");
                return 0;
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Console.WriteLine();
                Console.WriteLine("FATAL ERROR:");
                Console.WriteLine(ex.ToString());
                return 1;
            }
        }

        private static void PrintUsage()
        {
            Console.WriteLine("Usage:");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe <sourceDll> <sourceRuntimeDir> <destinationDir>");
            Console.WriteLine();
            Console.WriteLine("Example:");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe");
            Console.WriteLine("    \"E:\\repo\\rust-src\\target\\dll-release\\i686-rust9x-windows-msvc\\release\\rust9x_windows_auth.dll\"");
            Console.WriteLine("    \"E:\\repo\\rust-src\\target\\dll-release\\i686-rust9x-windows-msvc\\release\"");
            Console.WriteLine("    \"E:\\repo\\net-framework-gui\\Brutus\\HandlerGui\\bin\\Debug\"");
        }

        private static string NormalizePath(string path)
        {
            if (path == null)
                return string.Empty;

            path = path.Trim().Trim('"');

            if (path.Length == 0)
                return string.Empty;

            try
            {
                return Path.GetFullPath(path);
            }
            catch
            {
                return path;
            }
        }

        private static void DumpDirectory(string label, string dir)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== " + label + " ===");

            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(dir))
            {
                Console.WriteLine("(empty path)");
                return;
            }

            if (!Directory.Exists(dir))
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Missing: " + dir);
                return;
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Path: " + dir);

            string[] files = Directory.GetFiles(dir);
            Console.WriteLine("Files: " + files.Length);
            for (int i = 0; i < files.Length; i++)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("  " + Path.GetFileName(files[i]));
            }

            string crtDir = Path.Combine(dir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT");
            if (Directory.Exists(crtDir))
            {
                string[] crtFiles = Directory.GetFiles(crtDir);
                Console.WriteLine("CRT files: " + crtFiles.Length);
                for (int j = 0; j < crtFiles.Length; j++)
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("  Microsoft.VC80.CRT\\" + Path.GetFileName(crtFiles[j]));
                }
            }

            Console.WriteLine();
        }

        private static void CopyRuntimeFiles(string sourceRuntimeDir, string destinationDir)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== CopyRuntimeFiles ===");

            if (!Directory.Exists(sourceRuntimeDir))
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Runtime directory missing: " + sourceRuntimeDir);
                return;
            }

            string[] rootFiles = Directory.GetFiles(sourceRuntimeDir);
            for (int i = 0; i < rootFiles.Length; i++)
            {
                string file = rootFiles[i];
                string name = Path.GetFileName(file);

                string lower = name.ToLowerInvariant();
                if (lower.EndsWith(".pdb"))
                    continue;

                if (string.Compare(name, Path.GetFileName(destinationDir), true) == 0)
                    continue;

                string dest = Path.Combine(destinationDir, name);
                CopyFile(file, dest);
            }

            string crtSource = Path.Combine(sourceRuntimeDir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT");
            if (Directory.Exists(crtSource))
            {
                string crtDest = Path.Combine(destinationDir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT");
                if (!Directory.Exists(crtDest))
                    Directory.CreateDirectory(crtDest);

                string[] crtFiles = Directory.GetFiles(crtSource);
                for (int j = 0; j < crtFiles.Length; j++)
                {
                    string crtFile = crtFiles[j];
                    string crtDestFile = Path.Combine(crtDest, Path.GetFileName(crtFile));
                    CopyFile(crtFile, crtDestFile);
                }
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Runtime copy complete.");
        }

        private static void CopyFile(string source, string destination)
        {
            string destDir = Path.GetDirectoryName(destination);
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(destDir) && !Directory.Exists(destDir))
                Directory.CreateDirectory(destDir);

            Console.WriteLine("Copy: " + source);
            Console.WriteLine("  -> " + destination);

            File.Copy(source, destination, true);
        }
    }
} 
more  robust including file path walking  using cycles and not just praying that relative paths work - instead cycle through directories and levels and search the file system and do it complex

**ChatGPT** *(05.08.2026, 18:09:32)*

Yes. In fact I'd make the tool responsible for **resolving the paths**, not the MSBuild project. MSBuild path handling on VS2005/.NET 2.0 is surprisingly fragile because `$(ProjectDir)`, `$(SolutionDir)`, `WorkingDirectory`, relative paths and `Exec` all behave differently depending on whether the build comes from the IDE or MSBuild.exe.

Instead of expecting

```
..\..\..\rust-src\target\dll-debug\...
```

to already be correct, have BuildCopyTool locate everything itself.

I'd implement something like this:

1. **Normalize everything**
   - `Path.GetFullPath()`
   - remove quotes
   - resolve `.` and `..`

2. **Walk upward from every supplied path**
   For example if the project gives

```
E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\net-framework-gui\Brutus\HandlerGui\
```

walk

```
HandlerGui
Brutus
net-framework-gui
rust9x-windows2000auth
code
E:\
```

At every level look for

```
rust-src
target
BuildCopyTool
```

etc.

---

3. **Search recursively**

If DLL wasn't found exactly where expected:

```
SearchDirectory(
    root,
    "rust9x_windows_auth.dll",
    maxDepth:6);
```

Likewise

```
SearchDirectory(
    root,
    "Microsoft.VC80.CRT",
    maxDepth:6);
```

---

4. **Locate rust-src automatically**

Instead of trusting

```
..\..\..\rust-src
```

search upward:

```
while(current!=null)
{
    if(Directory.Exists(Path.Combine(current,"rust-src")))
        return Path.Combine(current,"rust-src");

    current=Directory.GetParent(current);
}
```

---

5. **Search by filename**

If

```
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

isn't found in the supplied location, search

```
rust-src\target
```

for

```
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

choosing the newest file.

```
Directory.GetFiles(
    root,
    dllName,
    SearchOption.AllDirectories);
```

Then

```
Array.Sort(...)
```

by LastWriteTime.

---

6. **Find runtime directory automatically**

If DLL is

```
...\target\dll-debug\...\debug\rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

runtime is simply

```
Path.GetDirectoryName(dll)
```

No need to pass runtime separately.

---

7. **Detect CRT**

Instead of assuming

```
Microsoft.VC80.CRT
```

exists beside DLL:

```
SearchDirectory(
    runtime,
    "Microsoft.VC80.CRT",
    directorySearch);
```

---

8. **Very verbose logging**

Print

```
CurrentDirectory
Executable
Arguments

Resolved DLL
Resolved runtime
Resolved output

Directories searched

Files examined

Reason chosen
```

For example

```
Searching:

E:\code\
E:\code\rust9x...
E:\code\rust9x...\rust-src
E:\code\rust9x...\rust-src\target
...

Found candidate:

...\dll-debug\...
Date:
Size:

Using newest candidate.
```

---

9. **Verify copied files**

After every copy

```
if(!File.Exists(dest))
    throw...
```

and compare

```
Length
LastWriteTime
```

---

10. **Mirror directories recursively**

Instead of hardcoding CRT

```
CopyDirectoryRecursive(
    runtime,
    output,
    ignorePdb:true);
```

That automatically copies

```
Microsoft.VC80.CRT
foo
bar
baz
```

if Rust ever starts producing additional folders.

---

11. **Automatic output detection**

If destination wasn't supplied or doesn't exist,

search upward for

```
bin\Debug
bin\Release
```

under

```
HandlerGui
Rust9xWindowsAuth
```

and use whichever matches the configuration.

---

12. **Configuration detection**

Instead of passing Debug/Release separately, infer it from the DLL path:

```
if(path.Contains("\\dll-debug\\"))
    Debug

if(path.Contains("\\dll-release\\"))
    Release
```

---

I'd actually reduce the command line to just:

```
BuildCopyTool.exe
    "$(ProjectDir)"
    "$(TargetDir)"
```

or even

```
BuildCopyTool.exe
    "$(MSBuildProjectDirectory)"
```

Then let the tool:

- locate `rust-src`
- locate `target`
- locate the newest `rust9x_windows_auth.dll`
- determine Debug/Release
- locate the runtime directory
- locate `Microsoft.VC80.CRT`
- copy everything recursively
- verify the copied files
- emit a complete diagnostic log

That makes the build almost immune to relative-path mistakes in the `.csproj`, because the executable discovers the project layout dynamically instead of relying on fragile MSBuild path expansion.

**ChatGPT** *(05.08.2026, 18:16:41)*

The main issue is that you're hooking into **`BuildDependsOn`**, which is not the right extension point for VS2005 C# projects. `Microsoft.CSharp.targets` defines the build pipeline, and overriding `BuildDependsOn` like this is unreliable.

Instead:

- Build `BuildCopyTool` **AfterBuild** (or BeforeBuild if you prefer).
- Run it **AfterBuild**.
- Don't invoke it via `BuildDependsOn`.

I'd replace the bottom section of your project with the following.

```xml
  <Import Project="$(MSBuildBinPath)\Microsoft.CSharp.targets" />

  <PropertyGroup>
    <AfterBuildDependsOn>
      $(AfterBuildDependsOn);
      BuildBuildCopyTool;
      RunBuildCopyTool
    </AfterBuildDependsOn>

    <CleanDependsOn>
      $(CleanDependsOn);
      CleanRustFiles
    </CleanDependsOn>
  </PropertyGroup>

  <Target Name="LogRustPaths">
    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="HandlerGui Build Information" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Configuration : $(Configuration)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Platform      : $(Platform)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="ProjectDir    : $(ProjectDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="TargetDir     : $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RustRoot      : $(RustProjectRoot)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Rust DLL      : $(RustDllSourcePath)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Runtime Dir   : $(RustRuntimeDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Copy Tool     : $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />
  </Target>

  <Target Name="BeforeBuild"
          DependsOnTargets="LogRustPaths">
  </Target>

  <Target Name="BuildBuildCopyTool">

    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Building BuildCopyTool..." Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />

    <MSBuild
        Projects="$(ProjectDir)..\BuildCopyTool\BuildCopyTool.csproj"
        Targets="Build"
        Properties="Configuration=$(Configuration);Platform=AnyCPU" />

  </Target>

  <Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool"
          DependsOnTargets="BuildBuildCopyTool">

    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="Running BuildCopyTool" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="==========================================" Importance="high" />

    <Message Text="Tool : $(BuildCopyToolExe)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="DLL  : $(RustDllSourcePath)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="RT   : $(RustRuntimeDir)" Importance="high" />
    <Message Text="OUT  : $(TargetDir)" Importance="high" />

    <Error
        Condition="!Exists('$(BuildCopyToolExe)')"
        Text="BuildCopyTool.exe not found: $(BuildCopyToolExe)" />

    <Exec
        Command="&quot;$(BuildCopyToolExe)&quot; &quot;$(ProjectDir)&quot; &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;"
        WorkingDirectory="$(ProjectDir)"
        IgnoreExitCode="false" />

  </Target>

  <Target Name="AfterBuild"
          DependsOnTargets="RunBuildCopyTool">
  </Target>

  <Target Name="CleanRustFiles">

    <Delete
        Files="$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)$(RustDllName)')" />

    <Delete
        Files="$(OutputPath)msvcr80.dll"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcr80.dll')" />

    <Delete
        Files="$(OutputPath)msvcp80.dll"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcp80.dll')" />

    <Delete
        Files="$(OutputPath)msvcm80.dll"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)msvcm80.dll')" />

    <Delete
        Files="$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT\*.*"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT')" />

    <RemoveDir
        Directories="$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT"
        Condition="Exists('$(OutputPath)Microsoft.VC80.CRT')" />

    <Message Text="Rust artifacts cleaned." Importance="high" />

  </Target>
```

### I would also simplify `BuildCopyTool`

Instead of

```text
BuildCopyTool.exe
    dllPath
    runtimeDir
    outputDir
```

I'd change it to

```text
BuildCopyTool.exe
    projectDirectory
    outputDirectory
```

For example:

```xml
<Exec
    Command="&quot;$(BuildCopyToolExe)&quot; &quot;$(ProjectDir)&quot; &quot;$(TargetDir)&quot;"
    WorkingDirectory="$(ProjectDir)" />
```

Then the executable can discover:

- `rust-src`
- `target`
- `dll-debug`
- `dll-release`
- the newest `rust9x_windows_auth.dll`
- `Microsoft.VC80.CRT`

by walking the directory tree itself, eliminating almost all fragile path logic from the `.csproj`. That approach is considerably more resilient when the solution is moved or the build is run from Visual Studio, `msbuild.exe`, or another working directory.

we made  build tool this because complex is better than simple
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.IO;

namespace BuildCopyTool
{
    internal static class Program
    {
        private const string DllName = "rust9x_windows_auth.dll";
        private const int MaxSearchDepth = 6;
        private const int MaxUpwardWalkLevels = 10;

        private static int Main(string[] args)
        {
            try
            {
                Console.WriteLine("=== BuildCopyTool (Enhanced) ===");
                Console.WriteLine();

                PrintEnvironmentInfo();
                Console.WriteLine();

                string projectDir = null;
                string targetDir = null;

                if (args.Length >= 2)
                {
                    projectDir = NormalizePath(args[0]);
                    targetDir = NormalizePath(args[1]);
                }
                else if (args.Length == 1)
                {
                    projectDir = NormalizePath(args[0]);
                }
                else
                {
                    projectDir = NormalizePath(Environment.CurrentDirectory);
                }

                Console.WriteLine("=== Path Resolution ===");
                string projectDirDisplay = projectDir;
                if (projectDirDisplay == null) projectDirDisplay = "(not provided)";
                Console.WriteLine("Project directory : " + projectDirDisplay);

                string targetDirDisplay = targetDir;
                if (targetDirDisplay == null) targetDirDisplay = "(not provided)";
                Console.WriteLine("Target directory  : " + targetDirDisplay);
                Console.WriteLine();

                string rustSrcDir = LocateRustSrcDirectory(projectDir);
                if (rustSrcDir == null)
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("ERROR: Could not locate rust-src directory");
                    return 3;
                }

                Console.WriteLine("Located rust-src    : " + rustSrcDir);
                Console.WriteLine();

                string targetBaseDir = Path.Combine(rustSrcDir, "target");
                if (!Directory.Exists(targetBaseDir))
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("ERROR: target directory not found: " + targetBaseDir);
                    return 4;
                }

                string dllPath = LocateNewestDll(targetBaseDir);
                if (dllPath == null)
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("ERROR: Could not locate " + DllName);
                    return 5;
                }

                Console.WriteLine("=== DLL Selection ===");
                Console.WriteLine("Selected DLL        : " + dllPath);
                Console.WriteLine("Last modified       : " + File.GetLastWriteTime(dllPath));
                FileInfo dllFileInfo = new FileInfo(dllPath);
                Console.WriteLine("Size                : " + dllFileInfo.Length + " bytes");
                Console.WriteLine();

                string configuration = DetectConfiguration(dllPath);
                Console.WriteLine("Detected configuration: " + configuration);
                Console.WriteLine();

                string runtimeDir = Path.GetDirectoryName(dllPath);
                Console.WriteLine("Runtime directory   : " + runtimeDir);
                Console.WriteLine();

                string destinationDir = targetDir;
                if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(destinationDir))
                {
                    destinationDir = LocateOutputDirectory(projectDir, configuration);
                    if (destinationDir == null)
                    {
                        Console.WriteLine("ERROR: Could not locate output directory");
                        return 6;
                    }
                }

                Console.WriteLine("Destination directory: " + destinationDir);
                Console.WriteLine();

                if (!Directory.Exists(destinationDir))
                {
                    Directory.CreateDirectory(destinationDir);
                    Console.WriteLine("Created destination directory.");
                    Console.WriteLine();
                }

                Console.WriteLine("=== Copying Files ===");
                string dllDest = Path.Combine(destinationDir, Path.GetFileName(dllPath));
                CopyFileWithVerification(dllPath, dllDest);

                CopyDirectoryRecursive(runtimeDir, destinationDir, true);

                Console.WriteLine();
                Console.WriteLine("=== BuildCopyTool completed successfully ===");
                return 0;
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Console.WriteLine();
                Console.WriteLine("=== FATAL ERROR ===");
                Console.WriteLine(ex.ToString());
                return 1;
            }
        }

        private static void PrintEnvironmentInfo()
        {
            Console.WriteLine("Current directory   : " + Environment.CurrentDirectory);
            Console.WriteLine("Executable          : " + Environment.GetCommandLineArgs()[0]);
            Console.WriteLine("Arguments           : " + string.Join(" ", Environment.GetCommandLineArgs()));
        }

        private static void PrintUsage()
        {
            Console.WriteLine("Usage:");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe [projectDir] [targetDir]");
            Console.WriteLine();
            Console.WriteLine("Arguments:");
            Console.WriteLine("  projectDir - Optional. Project directory containing .csproj. Defaults to current directory.");
            Console.WriteLine("  targetDir  - Optional. Output directory for DLL and runtime files. Auto-detected if not provided.");
            Console.WriteLine();
            Console.WriteLine("Examples:");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe \"E:\\code\\rust9x-windows2000auth\\net-framework-gui\\Brutus\\HandlerGui\"");
            Console.WriteLine("  BuildCopyTool.exe \"$(ProjectDir)\" \"$(TargetDir)\"");
        }

        private static string NormalizePath(string path)
        {
            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(path))
                return null;

            path = path.Trim().Trim('"');

            if (path.Length == 0)
                return null;

            try
            {
                return Path.GetFullPath(path);
            }
            catch
            {
                return path;
            }
        }

        private static string LocateRustSrcDirectory(string startPath)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== Locating rust-src ===");

            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(startPath))
                startPath = Environment.CurrentDirectory;

            DirectoryInfo current = new DirectoryInfo(startPath);
            int levelsWalked = 0;

            while (current != null && levelsWalked < MaxUpwardWalkLevels)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Checking: " + current.FullName);

                string rustSrcPath = Path.Combine(current.FullName, "rust-src");
                if (Directory.Exists(rustSrcPath))
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("Found rust-src at: " + rustSrcPath);
                    return rustSrcPath;
                }

                current = current.Parent;
                levelsWalked++;
            }

            Console.WriteLine("rust-src not found after walking " + levelsWalked + " levels");
            return null;
        }

        private static string LocateNewestDll(string searchRoot)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== Searching for " + DllName + " ===");

            List<string> candidates = new List<string>();

            try
            {
                string[] allDlls = Directory.GetFiles(searchRoot, DllName, SearchOption.AllDirectories);
                candidates.AddRange(allDlls);
            }
            catch (UnauthorizedAccessException ex)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Warning: Access denied during search: " + ex.Message);
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Warning: Error during search: " + ex.Message);
            }

            if (candidates.Count == 0)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("No DLL candidates found");
                return null;
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Found " + candidates.Count + " candidate(s):");

            candidates.Sort(new DllFileComparer());

            int displayCount = 5;
            if (candidates.Count < displayCount)
                displayCount = candidates.Count;

            for (int i = 0; i < displayCount; i++)
            {
                FileInfo fi = new FileInfo(candidates[i]);
                Console.WriteLine("  [" + (i + 1) + "] " + candidates[i]);
                Console.WriteLine("       Date: " + fi.LastWriteTime + ", Size: " + fi.Length + " bytes");
            }

            if (candidates.Count > 5)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("  ... and " + (candidates.Count - 5) + " more");
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Using newest candidate: " + candidates[0]);
            return candidates[0];
        }

        private class DllFileComparer : IComparer<string>
        {
            public int Compare(string a, string b)
            {
                DateTime timeA = File.GetLastWriteTime(a);
                DateTime timeB = File.GetLastWriteTime(b);
                return timeB.CompareTo(timeA);
            }
        }

        private static string DetectConfiguration(string dllPath)
        {
            string lowerPath = dllPath.ToLowerInvariant();

            if (lowerPath.Contains("\\dll-debug\\") || lowerPath.Contains("/dll-debug/"))
                return "Debug";

            if (lowerPath.Contains("\\dll-release\\") || lowerPath.Contains("/dll-release/"))
                return "Release";

            if (lowerPath.Contains("\\debug\\") || lowerPath.Contains("/debug/"))
                return "Debug";

            if (lowerPath.Contains("\\release\\") || lowerPath.Contains("/release/"))
                return "Release";

            return "Unknown";
        }

        private static string LocateOutputDirectory(string projectDir, string configuration)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== Locating output directory ===");

            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(projectDir))
                projectDir = Environment.CurrentDirectory;

            DirectoryInfo current = new DirectoryInfo(projectDir);
            int levelsWalked = 0;

            while (current != null && levelsWalked < MaxUpwardWalkLevels)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Checking: " + current.FullName);

                string[] searchPatterns = { "HandlerGui", "Rust9xWindowsAuth" };

                foreach (string pattern in searchPatterns)
                {
                    string projectPath = Path.Combine(current.FullName, pattern);
                    if (Directory.Exists(projectPath))
                    {
                        Console.WriteLine("Found project: " + projectPath);

                        string binPath = Path.Combine(projectPath, "bin");
                        if (Directory.Exists(binPath))
                        {
                            string configPath = Path.Combine(binPath, configuration);
                            if (Directory.Exists(configPath))
                            {
                                Console.WriteLine("Found output directory: " + configPath);
                                return configPath;
                            }

                            string debugPath = Path.Combine(binPath, "Debug");
                            string releasePath = Path.Combine(binPath, "Release");

                            if (Directory.Exists(debugPath))
                            {
                                Console.WriteLine("Using Debug output: " + debugPath);
                                return debugPath;
                            }

                            if (Directory.Exists(releasePath))
                            {
                                Console.WriteLine("Using Release output: " + releasePath);
                                return releasePath;
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }

                current = current.Parent;
                levelsWalked++;
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Output directory not found after walking " + levelsWalked + " levels");
            return null;
        }

        private static void CopyDirectoryRecursive(string sourceDir, string destDir, bool ignorePdb)
        {
            Console.WriteLine("=== Copying directory recursively ===");
            Console.WriteLine("Source: " + sourceDir);
            Console.WriteLine("Destination: " + destDir);
            Console.WriteLine();

            if (!Directory.Exists(sourceDir))
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Source directory does not exist: " + sourceDir);
                return;
            }

            if (!Directory.Exists(destDir))
            {
                Directory.CreateDirectory(destDir);
            }

            CopyDirectoryRecursiveInternal(sourceDir, destDir, ignorePdb, 0);

            Console.WriteLine("Recursive copy complete.");
        }

        private static void CopyDirectoryRecursiveInternal(string sourceDir, string destDir, bool ignorePdb, int currentDepth)
        {
            if (currentDepth > MaxSearchDepth)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Warning: Maximum search depth reached at: " + sourceDir);
                return;
            }

            string[] files = Directory.GetFiles(sourceDir);
            foreach (string file in files)
            {
                string fileName = Path.GetFileName(file);
                string lowerFileName = fileName.ToLowerInvariant();

                if (ignorePdb && lowerFileName.EndsWith(".pdb"))
                {
                    Console.WriteLine("Skipping PDB: " + fileName);
                    continue;
                }

                string destFile = Path.Combine(destDir, fileName);
                CopyFileWithVerification(file, destFile);
            }

            string[] subDirs = Directory.GetDirectories(sourceDir);
            foreach (string subDir in subDirs)
            {
                string subDirName = Path.GetFileName(subDir);
                string destSubDir = Path.Combine(destDir, subDirName);

                if (!Directory.Exists(destSubDir))
                {
                    Directory.CreateDirectory(destSubDir);
                }

                CopyDirectoryRecursiveInternal(subDir, destSubDir, ignorePdb, currentDepth + 1);
            }
        }

        private static void CopyFileWithVerification(string source, string destination)
        {
            string destDir = Path.GetDirectoryName(destination);
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(destDir) && !Directory.Exists(destDir))
            {
                Directory.CreateDirectory(destDir);
            }

            Console.WriteLine("Copy: " + source);
            Console.WriteLine("  -> " + destination);

            File.Copy(source, destination, true);

            if (!File.Exists(destination))
            {
                throw new IOException("Copy verification failed: destination file does not exist");
            }

            FileInfo sourceInfo = new FileInfo(source);
            FileInfo destInfo = new FileInfo(destination);

            if (sourceInfo.Length != destInfo.Length)
            {
                throw new IOException("Copy verification failed: size mismatch (source: " + sourceInfo.Length + ", dest: " + destInfo.Length + ")");
            }

            TimeSpan timeDiff = (sourceInfo.LastWriteTime - destInfo.LastWriteTime).Duration();
            if (timeDiff.TotalSeconds > 2)
            {
                Console.WriteLine("Warning: Timestamp difference: " + timeDiff.TotalSeconds + " seconds");
            }

            Console.WriteLine("  Verified: " + destInfo.Length + " bytes");
        }
    }
}

**ChatGPT** *(05.08.2026, 18:17:22)*

I would actually avoid making it *that* autonomous.

The first half of the tool (directory walking, verification, logging) is excellent because it makes builds much more diagnosable.

The part I would change is this:

- searching the entire tree for the newest `rust9x_windows_auth.dll`
- guessing Debug vs Release
- guessing HandlerGui vs Rust9xWindowsAuth output folders

Those heuristics eventually bite you when multiple builds exist.

A better compromise is:

- MSBuild still tells the tool **where the project directory is** and **where the output directory is**.
- The tool discovers **rust-src** by walking upward.
- The tool discovers the DLL inside `rust-src/target`.
- The tool verifies everything.
- The tool recursively copies everything.
- If an explicit DLL path was passed and it exists, use it.
- Otherwise fall back to filesystem discovery.

That gives you robustness without guessing the wrong output.

---

## I'd also improve the search

Instead of

```csharp
Directory.GetFiles(root, DllName, SearchOption.AllDirectories);
```

I'd implement an actual recursive walker.

Advantages:

- survives UnauthorizedAccessException on one directory
- continues searching
- can log every directory
- can limit recursion depth
- can skip useless folders
- deterministic

Example:

```csharp
private static void FindFilesRecursive(
    string directory,
    string fileName,
    List<string> matches,
    int depth)
{
    if (depth > MaxSearchDepth)
        return;

    Console.WriteLine("Scanning: " + directory);

    try
    {
        foreach (string file in Directory.GetFiles(directory))
        {
            if (string.Compare(
                Path.GetFileName(file),
                fileName,
                true) == 0)
            {
                matches.Add(file);
            }
        }

        foreach (string sub in Directory.GetDirectories(directory))
        {
            string name = Path.GetFileName(sub);

            if (name.Equals(".git",
                    StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase))
                continue;

            if (name.Equals("obj",
                    StringComparison.OrdinalIgnoreCase))
                continue;

            FindFilesRecursive(
                sub,
                fileName,
                matches,
                depth + 1);
        }
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        Console.WriteLine(
            "Skipped: "
            + directory
            + " : "
            + ex.Message);
    }
}
```

instead of relying on `SearchOption.AllDirectories`.

---

## I'd also walk upward smarter

Instead of

```csharp
..\..\..\..
```

keep

```csharp
DirectoryInfo current = new DirectoryInfo(projectDir);

while(current != null)
{
    ...
    current=current.Parent;
}
```

but at every level search for

```
rust-src
```

```
BuildCopyTool
```

```
HandlerGui
```

```
Rust9xWindowsAuth
```

so the build survives moving the solution.

---

## I'd also verify the DLL

After finding candidates I'd print something like

```
Candidate 1

Path:
E:\...\target\dll-debug\...

Date:
2026-08-05 14:25

Size:
492 KB

Contains VC80 CRT:
YES

-------------------------
```

instead of only

```
Using newest candidate
```

---

## I'd also validate the runtime

Before copying:

```
Checking runtime...

✔ rust9x_windows_auth.dll

✔ Microsoft.VC80.CRT

✔ msvcr80.dll

✔ manifest

✔ xxxxx.dll

Runtime OK
```

or

```
Missing:

Microsoft.VC80.CRT
```

which immediately tells you why the executable won't start.

---

## I would also add hash verification

Instead of only

```csharp
Length
```

verify

```
SHA256(source)

SHA256(dest)
```

or at least CRC32.

Then the log becomes

```
Copy

Source

Hash

Destination

Hash

Verification OK
```

which makes it impossible for antivirus/locking issues to silently corrupt a copy.

---

Overall, the direction is good: moving the fragile path logic out of MSBuild and into C# makes debugging much easier. I would keep MSBuild responsible only for invoking the tool with the project and output directories, and let the tool handle discovery, logging, verification, recursive copying, and error reporting. That gives you a much more maintainable build system while avoiding brittle relative paths.

**ChatGPT** *(08.08.2026, 00:03:59)*

I actually think that's a reasonable architecture **if your goal is that the build should continue working even after the solution is moved or cloned to a different location.**

In fact, I'd take it even further. Instead of MSBuild telling the tool where everything is, I'd let **BuildCopyTool discover the entire workspace**. Then the `.csproj` becomes almost trivial.

For example:

```xml
<Target Name="RunBuildCopyTool" AfterTargets="Build">
    <Exec Command="&quot;$(ProjectDir)..\BuildCopyTool\bin\$(Configuration)\BuildCopyTool.exe&quot;" />
</Target>
```

No Rust paths.
No runtime paths.
No DLL paths.
No target paths.

Everything is discovered by the executable.

---

I'd structure the search into several phases.

### Phase 1 — Find the workspace root

Starting from

```
Environment.CurrentDirectory
```

or

```
AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory
```

walk upward until one of these is found:

```
rust-src
```

or

```
HandlerGui
Rust9xWindowsAuth
BuildCopyTool
```

or even

```
*.sln
```

Once enough markers are present, declare

```
WorkspaceRoot
```

and stop.

---

### Phase 2 — Build an index

Instead of repeatedly calling

```csharp
Directory.GetFiles(...)
```

walk the tree once.

Create something like

```csharp
class FileNode
{
    public string FullPath;
    public string Name;
    public long Size;
    public DateTime LastWrite;
}
```

Then

```csharp
List<FileNode> Files;
```

or

```csharp
Dictionary<string,List<FileNode>>
```

indexed by filename.

After that

```
Find rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

is just

```csharp
Index["rust9x_windows_auth.dll"]
```

instead of rescanning the filesystem.

---

### Phase 3 — Score candidates

Suppose five DLLs exist.

Instead of

> newest wins

calculate a score.

For example

```
+500

inside target\dll-debug

+500

inside target\dll-release

+300

contains Microsoft.VC80.CRT

+200

contains target triple

+100

newest timestamp

-1000

inside bin

-1000

inside obj

-1000

inside backup

-1000

inside old
```

Then choose the highest scoring candidate.

That is much more reliable than "latest modified".

---

### Phase 4 — Locate runtime automatically

Never assume

```
runtime = Path.GetDirectoryName(dll)
```

Instead verify

```
Microsoft.VC80.CRT

manifest

msvcr80.dll

```

If the runtime is incomplete,

search neighboring directories.

Sometimes Cargo layouts change.

---

### Phase 5 — Locate outputs

Instead of

```
HandlerGui/bin/Debug
```

search for

```
HandlerGui.exe
```

or

```
Rust9xWindowsAuth.dll
```

inside

```
bin
```

If multiple outputs exist

```
bin/Debug

bin/Release

bin/x86/Debug
```

score them exactly like DLL candidates.

---

### Phase 6 — Verify copy

I would verify

- size
- SHA-256
- timestamp
- file attributes
- readonly flag
- retry on sharing violations

with something like

```
Retry 1

Retry 2

Retry 3

Success
```

because antivirus software sometimes briefly locks newly built binaries.

---

### Phase 7 — Produce a diagnostic report

One thing I'd definitely add is a complete log such as:

```
Workspace
---------
E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth

Projects
--------
HandlerGui
Rust9xWindowsAuth
BuildCopyTool

Rust builds
-----------
dll-debug
dll-release

Candidates
----------
1)
...
Score 1810

2)
...
Score 1720

Chosen
------
...

Destination
-----------
...

Copied
------
rust9x_windows_auth.dll

msvcr80.dll

manifest

Microsoft.VC80.CRT

Verification
------------
SHA256 OK

Finished
--------
Success
```

If the build ever fails on another machine, this report immediately shows **what the tool found, why it chose a particular DLL, and exactly what it copied**.

For a build helper, a self-discovering approach like this can be quite robust because it minimizes hardcoded paths in the project files. The key is to make the discovery deterministic—using a well-defined search strategy and scoring system—rather than relying on whichever matching file happens to be found first.

[L1] {
[L2]   "content": "# rust9x Windows Authentication DLL\n\nWindows Authentication library for legacy Windows systems (Windows 2000, XP, 9x/ME) using rust9x. Provides NTLM authentication, TLS support, and Windows credential dialog integration for .NET Framework 2.0 applications.\n\n## Features\n\n- **NTLMv2 Authentication**: Pure Rust implementation using sspi-rs, compatible with legacy Windows\n- **TLS/HTTPS Support**: native-tls using Windows SChannel for secure connections (Windows 2000+)\n- **Windows Credential Dialog**: Native Windows credential prompt via CredUIPromptForCredentials\n- **HTTP Client**: Built-in HTTP client with automatic NTLM authentication flow\n- **.NET Interop**: C-compatible API for P/Invoke from .NET Framework 2.0\n- **Dual Build**: Can be built as both DLL (for .NET) and standalone EXE (for testing)\n\n## Architecture\n\n```\n.NET Framework 2.0 App\n         ↓ P/Invoke\nrust9x_windows_auth.dll (Rust)\n         ↓\n┌─────────────────────────────┐\n│  NTLM Authentication (sspi) │\n│  TLS/HTTPS (rustls)         │\n│  Windows API (CredUI)       │\n│  HTTP Client                │\n└─────────────────────────────┘\n         ↓\nASP.NET Core API Server (Modern System)\n```\n\n## Building\n\n### Prerequisites\n\n1. **rust9x toolchain**: Install and link the rust9x toolchain\n2. **Platform SDK**: Update paths in `.cargo/config.toml` to match your setup\n3. **For Windows 9x/ME**: Place `unicows.dll` alongside the DLL\n\n### Build Commands\n\n```bash\n# Build DLL for .NET interop (release, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\n# NOTE: This builds ONLY the DLL, not the test EXE\ncargo +rust9x dll-release\n\n# Build DLL for .NET interop (debug, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\n# NOTE: This builds ONLY the DLL, not the test EXE\ncargo +rust9x dll-debug\n\n# Build test harness EXE (release, dynamic CRT - requires MSVCR on target)\ncargo +rust9x exe-release\n\n# Build test harness EXE (debug, dynamic CRT - requires MSVCR on target)\ncargo +rust9x exe-debug\n\n# Build test harness EXE (release, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\ncargo +rust9x exe-static-release\n\n# Build test harness EXE (debug, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\ncargo +rust9x exe-static-debug\n```\n\nOr use the full cargo commands:\n\n```bash\n# Build DLL for .NET interop (release, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\n# NOTE: This builds ONLY the DLL, not the test EXE\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --release --lib --features \"std,network,tls,dll-build\"\n\n# Build DLL for .NET interop (debug, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\n# NOTE: This builds ONLY the DLL, not the test EXE\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --lib --features \"std,network,tls,dll-build\"\n\n# Build DLL for legacy systems (no_std, release, static CRT)\n# NOTE: This builds ONLY the DLL, not the test EXE\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --release --lib --features \"network,tls,dll-build\"\n\n# Build test harness EXE (release, dynamic CRT - requires MSVCR on target)\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --bin rust9x_auth_test --release --features \"std,network,tls,exe-build\"\n\n# Build test harness EXE (debug, dynamic CRT - requires MSVCR on target)\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --bin rust9x_auth_test --features \"std,network,tls,exe-build\"\n\n# Build test harness EXE (release, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --bin rust9x_auth_test --release --features \"std,network,tls,exe-static-build\"\n\n# Build test harness EXE (debug, static CRT - no MSVCR dependency on target)\ncargo +rust9x build --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc --bin rust9x_auth_test --features \"std,network,tls,exe-static-build\"\n```\n\n### Output Files\n\nEach build variant outputs to a separate directory:\n\n- `target/dll-release/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/release/rust9x_windows_auth.dll` - DLL (release, static CRT)\n- `target/dll-debug/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/debug/rust9x_windows_auth.dll` - DLL (debug, static CRT)\n- `target/exe-release/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/release/rust9x_auth_test.exe` - EXE (release, dynamic CRT)\n- `target/exe-debug/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/debug/rust9x_auth_test.exe` - EXE (debug, dynamic CRT)\n- `target/exe-static-release/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/release/rust9x_auth_test.exe` - EXE (release, static CRT)\n- `target/exe-static-debug/i686-rust9x-windows-msvc/debug/rust9x_auth_test.exe` - EXE (debug, static CRT)\n\nEach output directory also includes:\n- `Microsoft.VC80.CRT/msvcr80.dll` - Visual C++ 2005 runtime (SxS assembly)\n- `Microsoft.VC80.CRT/Microsoft.VC80.CRT.manifest` - Assembly manifest\n- `msvcr80.dll` - Flat layout fallback for XP and below\n- `msvcr80.dll.manifest` - DLL manifest for flat layout\n\n## .NET Framework 2.0 Integration\n\nSee `NET_INTEROP.cs` for complete C# P/Invoke declarations and usage example.\n\n## Legacy Windows Compatibility\n\n| Windows Version | NTLM | TLS | Notes |\n|----------------|------|-----|-------|\n| Windows 95/98/ME | ✓ | ✗ | Requires unicows.dll |\n| Windows NT 4.0 | ✓ | ✗ | No TLS support |\n| Windows 2000 | ✓ | ✓ | Full support |\n| Windows XP | ✓ | ✓ | Full support |\n| Windows Vista+ | ✓ | ✓ | Full support |\n\n## Credits\n\n- **rust9x**: https://github.com/rust9x/rust - **ChatGPT** *(08.08.2026, 00:08:30)*

I checked the repo and the recent history. The main cause of the C# load failure is still the native-loading model: `rust-src/NET_INTEROP.cs` uses plain `[DllImport("rust9x_windows_auth.dll")]` for every entry point, so the CLR only finds the DLL if it is already in the process search path. That works when the DLL is copied next to the EXE, but it fails as soon as another C# app references the managed library and the native DLL is not copied into that app’s output folder. `AuthManager.PromptForCredentials()` also loses the updated save-checkbox value because it passes a local `bool` by ref to native and never returns the changed value to the caller. fileciteturn17file0turn26file0turn31file0

The build side is also split in a way that causes that exact problem: `HandlerGui.csproj` runs `BuildCopyTool`, but `Rust9xWindowsAuth.csproj` does not. So the native DLL gets staged for `HandlerGui`, not for every consumer of `Rust9xWindowsAuth`. A different C# project can reference the managed library successfully and still fail at first native call because the DLL was never copied into its `bin\...` folder. `BuildCopyTool` itself is already doing recursive search/copy and path walking, but it is only wired into the app project, not the library project. fileciteturn13file0turn14file0turn25file0turn7file0

On the Rust side, the packaging story is consistent: `build.rs` copies `msvcr80.dll`, `msvcp80.dll`, `msvcm80.dll`, and a generated or source manifest into `target\...\Microsoft.VC80.CRT`, and the README documents that each build output also contains flat fallback files. That means the output tree is intentionally noisy, and the current recursive-copy approach in `BuildCopyTool` will pull a lot of cargo byproducts into the managed app output unless it is narrowed to a whitelist. fileciteturn12file0turn23file0turn20file0

The commit history shows the project is still in a very active, rapidly changing state. One important change was commit `6ba005...`, which switched the DLL build strategy to static CRT and updated the documented build commands; later history then introduced a lot of IDE/build artifacts into the repository, including `.suo` files and a large `HandlerGui/bin/Debug` tree. That is a repo hygiene bug, and it makes stale binaries and wrong-copy behavior more likely. fileciteturn21file0

What I would fix next is this: make the native loader autonomous inside `Rust9xWindowsAuth` itself, so the managed wrapper resolves the DLL from its own assembly directory or uses an explicit `LoadLibrary` bootstrap before any `DllImport` call. That removes the dependency on whichever app happens to reference the library. I would also change the credential prompt API so the save-checkbox state is returned cleanly, and I would narrow `BuildCopyTool` to copy only the needed runtime files instead of recursively mirroring the whole target tree.

**ChatGPT** *(08.08.2026, 00:09:13)*

Да. Теперь видно гораздо точнее, где проблема.

Я посмотрел `CentralFile.cs`, `rust-src/src/lib.rs`, `Cargo.toml` и `.cargo/config.toml`. В текущем варианте проблема **не сводится к тому, что C# "не находит" `rust9x_windows_auth.dll`**. Вы уже обходите обычный P/Invoke loader и сами вызываете `LoadLibrary`. Это правильное направление для такого старого стека, но сейчас loader слишком оптимистично считает успешной загрузку самого DLL. fileciteturn32file0

### Главная проблема

У вас Rust DLL собирается как `cdylib` под:

```text
i686-rust9x-windows-msvc
```

и C# приложение при этом собирается как:

```text
AnyCPU
```

Это очень опасная комбинация.

Если `HandlerGui.exe` запускается 64-битным CLR, `LoadLibrary()` закономерно даст:

```text
ERROR_BAD_EXE_FORMAT = 193
```

То есть **C# процесс должен быть x86**, независимо от того, что проект называется `AnyCPU`.

В `HandlerGui.csproj` я бы первым делом сделал:

```xml
<PlatformTarget>x86</PlatformTarget>
```

и для Debug, и для Release.

Иначе вся эта система поиска DLL может быть абсолютно идеальной, а Windows всё равно откажется загружать i686 DLL.

---

## Но есть ещё более интересная проблема

В `CentralFile.cs` сейчас:

```csharp
_dllHandle = LoadLibrary(dllPath);
```

После этого вы считаете DLL загруженной:

```csharp
_isInitialized = true;
```

Но **не проверяете её зависимости до начала вызова функций**.

А Rust configuration показывает, что DLL строится с очень специфичным старым MSVC/Windows 9x окружением:

```text
i686-rust9x-windows-msvc
VC8
PlatformSDK
secur32.lib
ws2_32.lib
unicows.lib
```

и при этом у вас отдельно существует логика static CRT. fileciteturn36file0

Поэтому:

```text
LoadLibrary(rust9x_windows_auth.dll)
```

может закончиться:

```text
ERROR_MOD_NOT_FOUND (126)
```

хотя **сам `rust9x_windows_auth.dll` существует**.

И это как раз классическая ситуация:

```text
C# application
    |
    +-- rust9x_windows_auth.dll
            |
            +-- dependency A
            +-- dependency B
            +-- dependency C
```

Windows говорит `126`, но сообщение означает не обязательно отсутствие самого `rust9x_windows_auth.dll`; это может быть отсутствие **одной из зависимостей**.

Ваш текущий `GetLoadLibraryErrorMessage()` это частично понимает, потому что для 126 уже пишет:

> missing dependencies or wrong architecture

Но дальше диагностики нет. fileciteturn32file0

---

# И я бы изменил саму архитектуру `CentralFile.cs`

Сейчас у вас:

```text
FindDllPath()
    ↓
LoadLibrary()
    ↓
GetProcAddress() для каждой функции
```

Я бы сделал:

```text
InitializeDll()
    ↓
DetectProcessArchitecture()
    ↓
FindCandidateDlls()
    ↓
Inspect PE architecture
    ↓
Try LoadLibrary(candidate)
    ↓
если 126:
    ↓
diagnostic dependency probing
    ↓
если loaded:
    ↓
Resolve ALL exports once
    ↓
Validate ABI
    ↓
mark initialized
```

То есть **не считать DLL initialized, пока не проверены exports**.

---

# Ещё один реальный баг в Rust

Вот это:

```rust
pub static mut AUTH_CLIENT: Option<WindowsAuthClient> = None;
```

а дальше практически все exported functions делают:

```rust
unsafe {
    if AUTH_CLIENT.is_none() {
        ...
    }

    let client = AUTH_CLIENT.as_mut().unwrap();
}
```

Это не просто косметическая проблема.

Если C# GUI вызовет, например:

```text
auth_http_request()
```

из другого потока одновременно с:

```text
auth_cleanup()
```

или двумя HTTP requests одновременно, вы получаете гонки вокруг глобального mutable state.

Для WinForms это вполне реально.

Лучше заменить на что-нибудь вроде `Mutex<Option<WindowsAuthClient>>`, но здесь надо учитывать ограничения старого Rust/target.

---

# Ещё один очень плохой момент

В `lib.rs` лог:

```rust
let log_path = "E:\\code\\rust9x-windows2000auth\\rust-src\\lib_log.txt";
```

Это надо убрать.

Сейчас DLL, собранная на вашей машине, **жёстко знает путь исходников разработчика**.

На другой машине:

```text
E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\
```

может вообще не существовать.

И получается:

```text
C# → DLL
       ↓
       log_to_file()
       ↓
       E:\code\...
```

Вместо этого путь должен вычисляться относительно DLL/app directory либо передаваться из C#.

---

# И вот ещё одна потенциальная проблема ABI

В Rust:

```rust
pub struct AuthInteropResult {
    pub error_code: AuthErrorCode,
    pub error_message: *mut c_char,
    pub response_data: *mut u8,
    pub response_length: usize,
}
```

C#:

```csharp
[StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential)]
public struct AuthInteropResult
{
    public AuthErrorCode error_code;
    public IntPtr error_message;
    public IntPtr response_data;
    public UIntPtr response_length;
}
```

Это в целом правильное соответствие для x86.

Но я бы **не оставлял `usize` в ABI без явного документирования**. Лучше ABI делать явно 32/64-bit predictable:

Rust:

```rust
pub struct AuthInteropResult {
    pub error_code: AuthErrorCode,
    pub error_message: *mut c_char,
    pub response_data: *mut u8,
    pub response_length: u32,
}
```

и C#:

```csharp
public UInt32 response_length;
```

если библиотека гарантированно рассчитана именно на x86.

Поскольку ваш target — `i686`, это вполне разумно.

---

# Самая серьёзная проблема в C# interop

У вас правильно указано:

```csharp
[UnmanagedFunctionPointer(
    CallingConvention.Cdecl,
    CharSet = CharSet.Ansi)]
```

Это хорошо.

Но я бы **явно указал CallingConvention для абсолютно всех delegate types**, включая те, где сейчас его нет.

Например:

```csharp
[UnmanagedFunctionPointer(CallingConvention.Cdecl)]
private delegate AuthErrorCode auth_init_delegate();
```

и:

```csharp
[UnmanagedFunctionPointer(CallingConvention.Cdecl)]
private delegate void auth_cleanup_delegate();
```

Потому что Rust:

```rust
pub extern "C" fn
```

означает C ABI.

Для старого x86 Windows это особенно важно.

---

# И ещё одна архитектурная проблема

Сейчас для каждого вызова:

```csharp
GetProcAddress(_dllHandle, "auth_http_request");
Marshal.GetDelegateForFunctionPointer(...)
```

То есть функция ищется заново.

Я бы сделал один раз:

```text
InitializeDll
    |
    +-- auth_init
    +-- auth_cleanup
    +-- auth_free_string
    +-- auth_free_data
    +-- auth_set_credentials
    +-- auth_http_request
    +-- auth_prompt_credentials
```

и сохранил delegates в static fields.

Например:

```csharp
private static auth_init_delegate _auth_init;
private static auth_cleanup_delegate _auth_cleanup;
private static auth_free_string_delegate _auth_free_string;
private static auth_free_data_delegate _auth_free_data;
private static auth_set_credentials_delegate _auth_set_credentials;
private static auth_http_request_delegate _auth_http_request;
private static auth_prompt_credentials_delegate _auth_prompt_credentials;
```

Тогда initialization становится настоящей проверкой ABI:

```text
LoadLibrary succeeded
        ↓
GetProcAddress(auth_init)      OK
GetProcAddress(auth_cleanup)   OK
GetProcAddress(auth_free_string) OK
GetProcAddress(auth_free_data) OK
GetProcAddress(auth_set_credentials) OK
GetProcAddress(auth_http_request) OK
GetProcAddress(auth_prompt_credentials) OK
        ↓
DLL READY
```

Если хоть один export отсутствует — **DLL не считается загруженной**.

---

# И BuildCopyTool у вас имеет смысл именно в этой архитектуре

Я согласен с вашим решением не полагаться на MSBuild-relative paths.

Сейчас цепочка должна быть:

```text
Visual Studio
    |
    +-- build Rust DLL вручную/отдельным build step
    |
    +-- build C# projects
    |
    +-- BuildCopyTool.exe
             |
             +-- самостоятельно найти rust-src
             |
             +-- самостоятельно найти target
             |
             +-- найти наиболее свежий
             |      rust9x_windows_auth.dll
             |
             +-- определить Debug/Release
             |
             +-- скопировать DLL
             |
             +-- скопировать runtime
             |
             +-- проверить размеры
             |
             +-- проверить destination
```

Это гораздо устойчивее, чем:

```xml
$(ProjectDir)..\..\..\..\rust-src
```

потому что ваш проект уже показал, насколько быстро такая относительная арифметика превращается в ад.

---

## Но я бы изменил BuildCopyTool ещё в одном месте

Сейчас он выбирает:

```csharp
LocateNewestDll(targetBaseDir)
```

То есть если в `target` лежат:

```text
target/
    dll-debug/
        ...
            rust9x_windows_auth.dll

    dll-release/
        ...
            rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

он просто выбирает **самый свежий файл**.

Это опасно.

Представьте:

```text
17:00 dll-release
17:05 dll-debug
```

Вы собираете Release → получите Debug DLL.

Гораздо правильнее:

```text
requested configuration
        ↓
dll-debug / dll-release
        ↓
target triple
        ↓
DLL
        ↓
newest candidate
```

А если configuration невозможно определить — лучше **остановиться с ошибкой**, чем молча выбрать случайную DLL.

---

# Что я бы исправил прямо сейчас

### 1. HandlerGui

```xml
<PlatformTarget>x86</PlatformTarget>
```

обязательно.

### 2. Rust9xWindowsAuth

Тоже:

```xml
<PlatformTarget>x86</PlatformTarget>
```

хотя для class library это менее критично — критичен конечный EXE.

### 3. CentralFile

Переделать loader на:

```text
architecture validation
→ candidate discovery
→ LoadLibrary
→ GetProcAddress ALL exports
→ cache delegates
→ initialized
```

### 4. Rust

Убрать:

```rust
"E:\\code\\rust9x-windows2000auth\\rust-src\\lib_log.txt"
```

и сделать runtime-relative logging.

### 5. Rust global state

Убрать голый:

```rust
static mut AUTH_CLIENT
```

в пользу синхронизированного состояния.

### 6. BuildCopyTool

Не выбирать DLL только по timestamp.

Выбирать:

```text
Debug → dll-debug
Release → dll-release
```

и только внутри правильного дерева искать DLL.

### 7. Самое главное для текущего `LoadLibrary` failure

Нужно различить:

```text
193 = wrong architecture
126 = dependency missing
127 = export/procedure missing
1114 = DLL initialization failed
5   = access denied
```

У вас уже есть часть этой диагностики, но её надо довести до конца. `CentralFile.cs` сейчас уже является фактически собственным native loader'ом, поэтому имеет смысл сделать его **полноценным диагностическим loader'ом**, а не наполовину P/Invoke wrapper / наполовину loader. fileciteturn32file0

И ещё: ваш Rust `lib.rs` действительно экспортирует `auth_init`, `auth_cleanup`, `auth_free_string`, `auth_free_data`, `auth_set_credentials`, `auth_http_request` и `auth_prompt_credentials` через `#[no_mangle] extern "C"`, то есть схема с `GetProcAddress` принципиально совместима с тем, что Rust сейчас собирает. fileciteturn35file0

Если текущая ошибка именно **«C# не может загрузить Rust DLL»**, я бы ставил приоритет так:

**№1 — x86/x64 mismatch конечного `HandlerGui.exe`;  
№2 — отсутствующая native dependency;  
№3 — неправильная конфигурация/не тот DLL из `target`;  
№4 — отсутствие нужного export;  
№5 — уже собственно ошибка Rust ABI.**

При этом `Cargo.toml` подтверждает, что DLL у вас действительно является `cdylib`, а aliases `dll-debug`/`dll-release` кладут результаты в отдельные `target/dll-*` деревья. fileciteturn34file0

urlРепозиторий rust9x-windows2000authhttps://github.com/pieckenst/rust9x-windows2000auth

**ChatGPT** *(08.08.2026, 00:18:41)*

These files are much better now. The solution is consistently `x86`, which is the right move for an `i686-rust9x-windows-msvc` DLL; your loader code already treats `193` as the architecture-mismatch case, so aligning the whole solution removes a major class of failure. The repo history also shows the build is actively being reshaped around x86 and DLL packaging, so this matches the current direction. fileciteturn32file0turn24file0turn21file0

The build split is sensible: `Rust9xWindowsAuth` now behaves like a managed wrapper project, while `HandlerGui` builds `BuildCopyTool` and runs it after build. That lines up with the separate `BuildCopyTool.csproj` console app. Two small fixes still stand out: quote `$(BuildCopyToolExe)` in the `Exec` command, and give that `Exec` a `WorkingDirectory="$(ProjectDir)"` so it is resilient if MSBuild runs from a different current directory. fileciteturn25file0turn24file0

The bigger runtime issue is still `CentralFile.cs`. `LoadLibrary()` succeeding is not enough; it should resolve and cache all required exports during initialization, then fail early if any export is missing. The current wrapper already has the right diagnostic shape for `126`, `127`, `193`, and `1114`, but it should promote missing exports to an initialization failure rather than discovering them later on first use. The credential-prompt helper also drops the checkbox state because the convenience method takes `bool save` by value, so that value never comes back to the caller. fileciteturn32file0turn31file0

On the Rust side, I would remove the hardcoded `E:\code\...` log path and replace `static mut AUTH_CLIENT` with synchronized state; both are hazards once the DLL is loaded from a different machine or touched from multiple threads. The Rust crate is still a `cdylib` with separate DLL/EXE profiles and aliases, so the C# side should treat it as a strict ABI boundary and validate it aggressively at load time. fileciteturn35file0turn34file0

I can patch the two `.csproj` files and the loader wrapper into a cleaned-up version next.
