**You** *(08.08.2026, 00:53:54)*

here is central file code 
// .NET Framework 2.0+ P/Invoke declarations for rust9x\_windows\_auth.dll
// This file shows how to call the Rust DLL from C# / VB.NET
// Compatible with .NET Framework 2.0 and later versions
//
// ROBUST DLL LOADING:
// This implementation uses explicit DLL loading via LoadLibrary to provide:
// - Better error messages when DLL loading fails
// - Multiple search path fallbacks (app directory, assembly location, system PATH)
// - Architecture mismatch detection
// - Proper DLL handle management
// - Function pointer resolution with error checking
//
// USAGE:
// 1. Call WindowsAuth.InitializeDll() before any other operations
// 2. Use the helper methods (SetCredentials, HttpRequest, etc.) which handle errors gracefully
// 3. Call WindowsAuth.auth\_cleanup() when done with the library
// 4. Call WindowsAuth.UnloadDll() to explicitly unload the DLL (optional but recommended)
//
// DLL SEARCH ORDER:
// 1. Application directory (AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory)
// 2. Assembly location (same directory as the calling assembly)
// 3. Current working directory
// 4. "rust-runtime" subdirectory of application directory
// 5. Parent directory (for development scenarios)
// 6. System PATH (handled by LoadLibrary)
//
// ERROR HANDLING:
// - DllLoadException is thrown when DLL loading fails with detailed error messages
// - InvalidOperationException is thrown when functions fail due to DLL loading issues
// - All helper methods wrap native calls with proper exception handling

using System;
using System.Runtime.InteropServices;
using System.Text;
using System.IO;
using System.Reflection;
using System.Diagnostics;

namespace Rust9xWindowsAuth
{
    /// \<summary>
    /// Error codes returned by the Rust authentication library
    /// \</summary>
    public enum AuthErrorCode
    {
        Success = 0,
        InvalidCredentials = 1,
        NetworkError = 2,
        TlsError = 3,
        AuthFailed = 4,
        InvalidParameter = 5,
        NotInitialized = 6,
        Unknown = -1
    }

    /// \<summary>
    /// Result structure for authentication operations
    /// \</summary>
    [StructLayout(LayoutKind.Sequential)]
    public struct AuthInteropResult
    {
        public AuthErrorCode error\_code;
        public IntPtr error\_message;
        public IntPtr response\_data;
        public UIntPtr response\_length;
    }

    /// \<summary>
    /// Exception thrown when DLL loading fails
    /// \</summary>
    public class DllLoadException : Exception
    {
        public DllLoadException(string message) : base(message) { }
        public DllLoadException(string message, Exception innerException) : base(message, innerException) { }
    }

    /// \<summary>
    /// P/Invoke wrapper for rust9x\_windows\_auth.dll with robust DLL loading
    /// \</summary>
    public static class WindowsAuth
    {
        private const string DLL\_NAME = "rust9x\_windows\_auth.dll";
        private static IntPtr \_dllHandle = IntPtr.Zero;
        private static bool \_isInitialized = false;
        private static readonly object \_initLock = new object();

        \#region Windows API for DLL Loading

        [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true, CharSet = CharSet.Auto)]
        private static extern IntPtr LoadLibrary(string lpFileName);

        [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true)]
        private static extern bool FreeLibrary(IntPtr hModule);

        [DllImport("kernel32.dll", SetLastError = true, CharSet = CharSet.Auto)]
        private static extern IntPtr GetProcAddress(IntPtr hModule, string lpProcName);

        \#endregion

        \#region DLL Loading

        /// \<summary>
        /// Initialize the DLL and load it explicitly
        /// \</summary>
        /// \<returns>True if DLL was loaded successfully, false otherwise\</returns>
        public static bool InitializeDll()
        {
            lock (\_initLock)
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: Starting DLL initialization");
                
                if (\_isInitialized && \_dllHandle != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: DLL already loaded, handle: " + \_dllHandle);
                    return true;
                }

                Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: Searching for DLL: " + DLL\_NAME);
                string dllPath = FindDllPath();
                
                if (dllPath == null)
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: DLL not found in any search path");
                    throw new DllLoadException(
                        "Could not find " + DLL\_NAME + ". " +
                        "Searched in: application directory, assembly location, and system PATH.");
                }

                Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: Attempting to load DLL from: " + dllPath);
                \_dllHandle = LoadLibrary(dllPath);
                
                if (\_dllHandle == IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    int error = Marshal.GetLastWin32Error();
                    Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: LoadLibrary failed with error code: " + error);
                    throw new DllLoadException(
                        "Failed to load DLL from '" + dllPath + "'. Windows error code: " + error + ". " +
                        GetLoadLibraryErrorMessage(error));
                }

                \_isInitialized = true;
                Trace.WriteLine("InitializeDll: DLL loaded successfully, handle: " + \_dllHandle);
                return true;
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Find the DLL in various locations
        /// \</summary>
        private static string FindDllPath()
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("FindDllPath: Starting DLL search");
            
            string[] searchPaths = new string[]
            {
                // 1. Application directory
                AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory,
                
                // 2. Assembly location (for DLL in same directory as the assembly)
                Path.GetDirectoryName(Assembly.GetExecutingAssembly().Location),
                
                // 3. Current directory
                Directory.GetCurrentDirectory(),
                
                // 4. Relative 'rust-runtime' subdirectory
                Path.Combine(AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory, "rust-runtime"),
                
                // 5. Parent directory (for development scenarios)
                Path.GetDirectoryName(AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory),
                
                // 6. System PATH will be searched automatically by LoadLibrary
                null
            };

            int pathIndex = 0;
            foreach (string basePath in searchPaths)
            {
                pathIndex++;
                if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(basePath))
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("FindDllPath: Path " + pathIndex + " is null, will search system PATH");
                    continue;
                }

                string dllPath = Path.Combine(basePath, DLL\_NAME);
                Trace.WriteLine("FindDllPath: Checking path " + pathIndex + ": " + dllPath);
                
                if (File.Exists(dllPath))
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("FindDllPath: DLL found at: " + dllPath);
                    return dllPath;
                }
            }

            Trace.WriteLine("FindDllPath: DLL not found in any search path, will use system PATH");
            // Return null to let LoadLibrary search system PATH
            return null;
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Get a human-readable error message for LoadLibrary failure
        /// \</summary>
        private static string GetLoadLibraryErrorMessage(int errorCode)
        {
            switch (errorCode)
            {
                case 126:
                    return "The specified module could not be found (missing dependencies or wrong architecture).";
                case 127:
                    return "The specified procedure could not be found.";
                case 5:
                    return "Access is denied.";
                case 1114:
                    return "DLL initialization routine failed.";
                case 193:
                    return "The DLL is 32-bit and the application is 64-bit (or vice versa). Architecture mismatch.";
                default:
                    return "Unknown error (code: " + errorCode + ").";
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Check if the DLL is loaded and available
        /// \</summary>
        public static bool IsDllLoaded
        {
            get { return \_isInitialized && \_dllHandle != IntPtr.Zero; }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Get the DLL handle (for advanced scenarios)
        /// \</summary>
        public static IntPtr DllHandle
        {
            get { return \_dllHandle; }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Unload the DLL explicitly
        /// \</summary>
        public static void UnloadDll()
        {
            lock (\_initLock)
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("UnloadDll: Starting DLL unload");
                
                if (\_dllHandle != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("UnloadDll: Freeing DLL handle: " + \_dllHandle);
                    bool result = FreeLibrary(\_dllHandle);
                    Trace.WriteLine("UnloadDll: FreeLibrary result: " + result);
                    
                    \_dllHandle = IntPtr.Zero;
                    \_isInitialized = false;
                    Trace.WriteLine("UnloadDll: DLL unloaded successfully");
                }
                else
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("UnloadDll: DLL handle is already zero, nothing to unload");
                }
            }
        }

        \#endregion

        \#region DLL Imports with Robust Loading

        /// \<summary>
        /// Helper method to ensure DLL is loaded before calling any function
        /// \</summary>
        private static void EnsureDllLoaded()
        {
            if (!\_isInitialized)
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("EnsureDllLoaded: DLL not initialized, calling InitializeDll");
                InitializeDll();
            }
            else
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("EnsureDllLoaded: DLL already initialized");
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Initialize the authentication library
        /// Must be called before any other operations
        /// \</summary>
        public static AuthErrorCode auth\_init()
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("auth\_init: Starting initialization");
            EnsureDllLoaded();
            
            IntPtr funcPtr = GetProcAddress(\_dllHandle, "auth\_init");
            if (funcPtr == IntPtr.Zero)
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("auth\_init: Could not find 'auth\_init' function in DLL");
                throw new DllLoadException("Could not find 'auth\_init' function in DLL");
            }

            Trace.WriteLine("auth\_init: Found function pointer for auth\_init");
            auth\_init\_delegate del = (auth\_init\_delegate)Marshal.GetDelegateForFunctionPointer(
                funcPtr, typeof(auth\_init\_delegate));
            
            AuthErrorCode result = del();
            Trace.WriteLine("auth\_init: Initialization completed with result: " + result);
            return result;
        }

        /// Helper to perform HTTP POST request with body
        /// \</summary>
        public static AuthResult HttpPost(string url, byte[] body)
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("HttpPost: Helper method called for POST request to: " + url + ", body length: " + (body != null ? body.Length.ToString() : "0"));
            return HttpRequest(url, "POST", body);
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Helper to perform HTTP request with method and optional body
        /// \</summary>
        public static AuthResult HttpRequest(string url, string method, byte[] body)
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("HttpRequest: Helper method called for " + method + " request to: " + url + ", body length: " + (body != null ? body.Length.ToString() : "0"));
            
            IntPtr bodyPtr = IntPtr.Zero;
            UIntPtr bodyLen = UIntPtr.Zero;

            if (body != null && body.Length > 0)
            {
                bodyPtr = Marshal.AllocHGlobal(body.Length);
                Marshal.Copy(body, 0, bodyPtr, body.Length);
                bodyLen = (UIntPtr)body.Length;
                Trace.WriteLine("HttpRequest: Allocated unmanaged memory for body at: " + bodyPtr);
            }

            try
            {
                AuthInteropResult result = auth\_http\_request(url, method, bodyPtr, bodyLen);
                Trace.WriteLine("HttpRequest: Request completed successfully");
                return new AuthResult(result);
            }
            catch (DllLoadException ex)
            {
                Trace.WriteLine("HttpRequest: DLL loading error: " + ex.Message);
                throw new InvalidOperationException(
                    "Failed to perform HTTP request due to DLL loading error: " + ex.Message, ex);
            }
            finally
            {
                if (bodyPtr != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    Marshal.FreeHGlobal(bodyPtr);
                    Trace.WriteLine("HttpRequest: Freed unmanaged memory for body");
                }
            }
        }

    /// \<summary>
    /// Wrapper for authentication results with proper disposal
    /// Implements IDisposable to ensure proper cleanup of native resources
    /// \</summary>
    public partial class AuthResult : IDisposable
    {
        private AuthInteropResult \_result;
        private bool \_disposed;

        public AuthResult(AuthInteropResult result)
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Constructor called");
            \_result = result;
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Error code from the operation
        /// \</summary>
        public AuthErrorCode ErrorCode
        {
            get 
            { 
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Getting ErrorCode: " + \_result.error\_code);
                return \_result.error\_code; 
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Error message (if any), automatically freed on disposal
        /// \</summary>
        public string ErrorMessage
        {
            get
            {
                if (\_result.error\_message != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    string message = Marshal.PtrToStringAnsi(\_result.error\_message);
                    Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Getting ErrorMessage: " + message);
                    return message;
                }
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: ErrorMessage is null");
                return null;
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Raw response data bytes (if any), automatically freed on disposal
        /// \</summary>
        public byte[] ResponseData
        {
            get
            {
                if (\_result.response\_data != IntPtr.Zero && \_result.response\_length != UIntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    byte[] data = new byte[(int)\_result.response\_length];
                    Marshal.Copy(\_result.response\_data, data, 0, (int)\_result.response\_length);
                    Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Getting ResponseData, length: " + data.Length);
                    return data;
                }
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: ResponseData is null");
                return null;
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Response data as UTF-8 string (if any)
        /// \</summary>
        public string ResponseString
        {
            get
            {
                byte[] data = ResponseData;
                if (data != null)
                {
                    string response = Encoding.UTF8.GetString(data);
                    Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Getting ResponseString, length: " + response.Length);
                    return response;
                }
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: ResponseString is null");
                return null;
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Whether the operation completed successfully
        /// \</summary>
        public bool Success
        {
            get 
            { 
                bool success = \_result.error\_code == AuthErrorCode.Success;
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Getting Success: " + success);
                return success; 
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Dispose pattern implementation
        /// \</summary>
        public void Dispose()
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Dispose called, disposed: " + \_disposed);
            if (!\_disposed)
            {
                if (\_result.error\_message != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Freeing error message at: " + \_result.error\_message);
                    WindowsAuth.auth\_free\_string(\_result.error\_message);
                }
                if (\_result.response\_data != IntPtr.Zero)
                {
                    Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Freeing response data at: " + \_result.response\_data + ", length: " + \_result.response\_length);
                    WindowsAuth.auth\_free\_data(\_result.response\_data, \_result.response\_length);
                }
                \_disposed = true;
                Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Dispose completed");
            }
            GC.SuppressFinalize(this);
        }

        \~AuthResult()
        {
            Trace.WriteLine("AuthResult: Finalizer called");
            Dispose();
        }
    }

    /// \<summary>
    /// Usage examples for the rust9x\_windows\_auth library
    /// \</summary>
    public class Example
    {
        /// \<summary>
        /// Helper method to write to both Console and Trace
        /// \</summary>
        private static void Log(string message)
        {
            Console.WriteLine(message);
            Trace.WriteLine(message);
        }

        public static void Main()
        {
            Log("=== rust9x Windows Auth .NET Interop Example ===\n");

            try
            {
                // Step 1: Load and initialize the DLL
                Log("[1] Loading authentication library DLL...");
                try
                {
                    WindowsAuth.InitializeDll();
                    Log("DLL loaded successfully");
                }
                catch (DllLoadException ex)
                {
                    Log("Failed to load DLL: " + ex.Message);
                    Log("Make sure rust9x\_windows\_auth.dll is in the application directory.");
                    return;
                }

                Log("[2] Initializing authentication library...");
                AuthErrorCode initResult = WindowsAuth.auth\_init();
                if (initResult != AuthErrorCode.Success)
                {
                    Log("Failed to initialize auth library: " + initResult);
                    return;
                }
                Log("Library initialized successfully\n");

                try
                {
                    // Step 2: Set credentials (choose one method)

                    // Method A: Set credentials programmatically
                    // Console.WriteLine("[2A] Setting credentials programmatically...");
                    // AuthErrorCode credsResult = WindowsAuth.SetCredentials("username", "password", "DOMAIN");
                    // if (credsResult != AuthErrorCode.Success)
                    // {
                    //     Console.WriteLine("Failed to set credentials: " + credsResult);
                    //     return;
                    // }
                    // Console.WriteLine("Credentials set successfully\n");

                    // Method B: Prompt for credentials using Windows dialog
                    Log("[2B] Prompting for credentials via Windows dialog...");
                    bool saveCredentials = false;
                    using (AuthResult promptResult = WindowsAuth.PromptCredentials(
                        "rust9x Windows Authentication",
                        "Enter your Windows credentials for NTLM authentication",
                        saveCredentials))
                    {
                        if (!promptResult.Success)
                        {
                            Log("Credential prompt failed: " + promptResult.ErrorMessage);
                            return;
                        }
                        Log("Credentials captured successfully");
                        Log("Save credentials: " + saveCredentials + "\n");
                    }

                    // Step 3: Perform HTTP request with NTLM authentication
                    Log("[3] Performing HTTP request with NTLM authentication...");
                    string targetUrl = "[http://example.com/api/test](http://example.com/api/test)"; // Replace with your target server

                    using (AuthResult httpResult = WindowsAuth.HttpRequest(targetUrl))
                    {
                        if (httpResult.Success)
                        {
                            Log("Request successful!");
                            Log("Response length: " +
                                (httpResult.ResponseData != null ? httpResult.ResponseData.Length.ToString() : "0") + " bytes");

                            if (httpResult.ResponseString != null)
                            {
                                Log("Response: " + httpResult.ResponseString);
                            }
                        }
                        else
                        {
                            Log("Request failed: " + httpResult.ErrorMessage);
                            Log("Error code: " + httpResult.ErrorCode);
                        }
                    }
                }
                finally
                {
                    // Step 4: Cleanup
                    Log("\n[4] Cleaning up library resources...");
                    WindowsAuth.auth\_cleanup();
                    Log("Cleanup complete");
                    
                    // Step 5: Unload DLL
                    Log("[5] Unloading DLL...");
                    WindowsAuth.UnloadDll();
                    Log("DLL unloaded");
                }
            }
            catch (DllLoadException ex)
            {
                Log("DLL Loading Error: " + ex.Message);
                Log("Stack trace: " + ex.StackTrace);
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Log("Exception: " + ex.Message);
                Log("Stack trace: " + ex.StackTrace);
            }

            Log("\nPress any key to exit...");
            Console.ReadKey();
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Example: NTLM token generation for custom protocol implementation
        /// \</summary>
        public static void NtlmTokenExample()
        {
            Log("=== NTLM Token Generation Example ===\n");

            try
            {
                // Load DLL
                Log("Loading DLL...");
                WindowsAuth.InitializeDll();
                Log("DLL loaded successfully");

                // Initialize
                AuthErrorCode initResult = WindowsAuth.auth\_init();
                if (initResult != AuthErrorCode.Success)
                {
                    Log("Initialization failed: " + initResult);
                    return;
                }

                try
                {
                    // Set credentials
                    WindowsAuth.SetCredentials("user", "password", "DOMAIN");

                    // Note: For direct NTLM token access, you would need to extend the Rust API
                    // Currently the library handles NTLM internally for HTTP requests
                    Log("NTLM authentication is handled internally for HTTP requests");
                    Log("Use HttpRequest() method for NTLM-authenticated HTTP calls");
                }
                finally
                {
                    WindowsAuth.auth\_cleanup();
                }
            }
            catch (DllLoadException ex)
            {
                Log("DLL Loading Error: " + ex.Message);
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Log("Exception: " + ex.Message);
            }
            finally
            {
                WindowsAuth.UnloadDll();
            }
        }

        /// \<summary>
        /// Example: Configuration with custom server
        /// \</summary>
        public static void CustomServerExample()
        {
            Log("=== Custom Server Configuration Example ===\n");

            try
            {
                // Load DLL
                Log("Loading DLL...");
                WindowsAuth.InitializeDll();
                Log("DLL loaded successfully");
            }
            catch (DllLoadException ex)
            {
                Log("Failed to load DLL: " + ex.Message);
                return;
            }

            try
            {
                // Initialize
                AuthErrorCode initResult = WindowsAuth.auth\_init();
                if (initResult != AuthErrorCode.Success)
                {
                    Log("Initialization failed: " + initResult);
                    return;
                }

                try
                {
                    // Configure your target server
                    string serverUrl = "[http://your-server.com/api/endpoint](http://your-server.com/api/endpoint)";
                    string method = "GET";

                    // Prompt for credentials
                    using (AuthResult promptResult = WindowsAuth.PromptCredentials())
                    {
                        if (!promptResult.Success)
                        {
                            Log("Credential prompt failed: " + promptResult.ErrorMessage);
                            return;
                        }
                    }

                    // Make request to custom server
                    using (AuthResult httpResult = WindowsAuth.HttpRequest(serverUrl, method, null))
                    {
                        if (httpResult.Success)
                        {
                            Log("Request to " + serverUrl + " succeeded");
                            Log("Response: " + httpResult.ResponseString);
                        }
                        else
                        {
                            Log("Request failed: " + httpResult.ErrorMessage);
                        }
                    }
                }
                finally
                {
                    WindowsAuth.auth\_cleanup();
                }
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                Log("Exception: " + ex.Message);
            }
            finally
            {
                // Unload DLL
                WindowsAuth.UnloadDll();
            }
        }
    }

**ChatGPT** *(08.08.2026, 00:53:54)*

This file is the right place to fix the loading problem. The overall direction is good: it uses explicit `LoadLibrary`, `GetProcAddress`, a custom `DllLoadException`, and it keeps the native API behind a managed wrapper instead of raw P/Invoke calls everywhere. The Rust side does export the functions you are resolving here (`auth_init`, `auth_cleanup`, `auth_free_string`, `auth_free_data`, `auth_set_credentials`, `auth_http_request`, `auth_prompt_credentials`), so the approach matches the DLL surface. fileciteturn32file0turn30file0turn31file0turn35file0

The first real bug is that the comments say the loader falls back to system PATH, but the code does not actually do that. `FindDllPath()` returns `null` when the file is not found in the explicit search folders, and then `InitializeDll()` throws before calling `LoadLibrary`. So PATH is never truly searched. If you want PATH fallback, `InitializeDll()` needs a second attempt with `LoadLibrary(DLL_NAME)` instead of failing immediately. fileciteturn33file0

The second problem is that `InitializeDll()` marks the DLL as loaded as soon as `LoadLibrary()` succeeds. That is not enough for this project, because `LoadLibrary()` can succeed while a dependency is missing or an export is absent. For this code, initialization should validate every required export up front and cache the delegates once, then only set `_isInitialized = true` after all lookups succeed. That gives you one clear failure point instead of discovering missing exports later on the first call. fileciteturn32file0turn35file0

The third bug is in the credential prompt helper. The low-level delegate correctly uses `ref bool saveCredentials`, but the convenience wrapper `PromptCredentials(string caption, string message, bool save)` takes the flag by value, so the caller never gets the updated checkbox state back. That is why the example code prints the original value after the prompt. The wrapper should also take `ref bool save`, or it should return a result object that includes the updated flag. fileciteturn31file0

Two more improvements are worth doing next. `AuthResult.Dispose()` currently frees native memory through the DLL, so `UnloadDll()` must not happen before all `AuthResult` instances are disposed. Also, all exported delegates should be explicitly marked `CallingConvention.Cdecl` to match the Rust `extern "C"` ABI. The Rust crate is built as a `cdylib`, so ABI consistency matters here. fileciteturn34file0turn35file0

The concrete fix order is: add a real PATH fallback, cache and validate all exports during `InitializeDll()`, change the prompt helper to preserve the `save` flag, and make unload/disposal order explicit. After that, the remaining load failures will be much easier to identify.

**ChatGPT** *(12.08.2026, 15:31:25)*

**ChatGPT** *(12.08.2026, 15:31:30)*

Now we have a much more conclusive diagnosis.

The important part of the debugger output is:

```text
FindDllPath: DLL found at:
E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\net-framework-gui\Brutus\HandlerGui\bin\Debug\rust9x_windows_auth.dll

InitializeDll: Attempting to load DLL from:
E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\net-framework-gui\Brutus\HandlerGui\bin\Debug\rust9x_windows_auth.dll

InitializeDll: LoadLibrary failed with error code: 126
```

So **the C# loader is working**. It found the DLL and passed the absolute path to `LoadLibrary()`. This is no longer a C# path problem.

`126` at this point means Windows could not load **one of the DLL's dependencies**. Your own `rust9x_windows_auth.dll` is present.

And there is a very important bug in the Rust build configuration that explains why.

## The static CRT configuration in `.cargo/config.toml` is not doing what you think

You currently have sections like:

```toml
[target.'cfg(all(target_family = "rust9x", target_env = "msvc", feature = "dll-build"))']
rustflags = [
    '-Ctarget-feature=+crt-static',
    '-Clink-arg=/NODEFAULTLIB:msvcrt.lib',
    '-Clink-arg=/NODEFAULTLIB:msvcrtd.lib',
    '-Clink-arg=libcmt.lib',
]
```

But Cargo explicitly does **not** allow Cargo features such as `feature = "dll-build"` in `target.'cfg(...)'` configuration matching. Cargo's documentation says the `cfg()` expression there is based on compiler target cfgs and explicitly says **not to match on Cargo features such as `feature="foo"`**. citeturn415868search0turn415868search1

So these sections:

```toml
cfg(... feature = "dll-build")
cfg(... feature = "exe-build")
cfg(... feature = "exe-static-build")
cfg(... feature = "network")
```

are not selecting the way you intended.

That means your aliases:

```toml
dll-release = [
    "build",
    "--target", "i686-rust9x-windows-msvc",
    "--release",
    "--lib",
    "--features", "dll-build,network,tls",
    "--target-dir", "target/dll-release"
]
```

do enable the **Rust crate features**, but they do not make the corresponding `target.'cfg(... feature = ...)'.rustflags` blocks activate. fileciteturn36file0

That is a major bug.

### What this means for your DLL

You intended:

```text
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
        ↓
static CRT
        ↓
no MSVCR80 dependency
```

But you may actually be getting:

```text
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
        ↓
dynamic CRT
        ↓
requires MSVCR80 / related VC runtime
```

The Rust Reference confirms that `+crt-static` is the correct mechanism for static CRT linkage on supported targets. citeturn415868search3turn415868search10

Your `build.rs` then additionally goes and copies the VC80 runtime into the output regardless, which makes the output look like it is deliberately supporting dynamic CRT even though the intended DLL configuration says otherwise. fileciteturn37file0

## There is another likely dependency: `unicows.dll`

Your `.cargo/config.toml` also has this as a normal linker argument:

```toml
'-Clink-arg=C:\\Program Files\\Microsoft Platform SDK\\Lib\\unicows.lib',
```

That is outside the broken feature-specific blocks. fileciteturn36file0

So the Rust DLL can acquire a dependency on the Microsoft Layer for Unicode (`unicows.dll`).

That is especially relevant because your target is deliberately:

```text
i686-rust9x-windows-msvc
```

and the project is explicitly linking against `unicows.lib`.

If `unicows.dll` is not installed/available to the process, Windows gives exactly the kind of failure you're seeing: the parent DLL exists, but `LoadLibrary()` returns `126` because a dependency cannot be resolved.

So I would investigate these dependencies in this order:

```text
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
    |
    +-- unicows.dll          <-- very suspicious
    |
    +-- MSVCR80.DLL          <-- likely if crt-static isn't actually active
    |
    +-- other native imports
```

The good news is that **your C# loader is already doing the correct thing by reporting `126` rather than blindly proceeding.**

# First thing to run

On the machine where this fails, from a Visual Studio command prompt, run:

```bat
dumpbin /DEPENDENTS "E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\net-framework-gui\Brutus\HandlerGui\bin\Debug\rust9x_windows_auth.dll"
```

That will tell us exactly what Windows expects the DLL to load.

Also run:

```bat
dumpbin /HEADERS "E:\code\rust9x-windows2000auth\net-framework-gui\Brutus\HandlerGui\bin\Debug\rust9x_windows_auth.dll" | findstr /I "machine"
```

It should report:


Given your current solution is `x86`, I expect that part to be correct.

# Add dependency diagnostics directly to `CentralFile.cs`

We can make your loader tell you much more than just:

```text
error 126
```

Before loading the Rust DLL, try the likely dependencies individually.

For example:

```csharp
private static void ProbeDependency(string path)
{
    Trace.WriteLine("Dependency probe: " + path);

    if (!File.Exists(path))
    {
        Trace.WriteLine("Dependency probe: FILE NOT FOUND");
        return;
    }

    IntPtr handle = LoadLibrary(path);

    if (handle == IntPtr.Zero)
    {
        int error = Marshal.GetLastWin32Error();

        Trace.WriteLine(
            "Dependency probe FAILED: " +
            path +
            ", error=" +
            error +
            ", message=" +
            GetLoadLibraryErrorMessage(error));

        return;
    }

    Trace.WriteLine(
        "Dependency probe OK: " +
        path +
        ", handle=" +
        handle);

    FreeLibrary(handle);
}
```

Then before loading Rust:

```csharp
ProbeDependency(
    Path.Combine(
        AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory,
        "msvcr80.dll"));

ProbeDependency(
    Path.Combine(
        AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory,
        "msvcp80.dll"));

ProbeDependency(
    Path.Combine(
        AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory,
        "msvcm80.dll"));

ProbeDependency(
    Path.Combine(
        AppDomain.CurrentDomain.BaseDirectory,
        "unicows.dll"));
```

That will immediately give us something like:

```text
Dependency probe: ...\msvcr80.dll
Dependency probe OK

Dependency probe: ...\msvcp80.dll
Dependency probe OK

Dependency probe: ...\unicows.dll
Dependency probe FAILED: error=126
```

and then we've got the smoking gun.

---

# Fix the Cargo configuration

I would **not** try to fix the current setup by making increasingly elaborate `cfg(feature = ...)` expressions.

Cargo explicitly says those feature checks don't work in target config matching. citeturn415868search1

Instead, make the target's base configuration correct and put the CRT selection directly into the actual build invocation.

For the DLL build:

```bat
cargo rustc ^
  --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc ^
  --release ^
  --lib ^
  --features dll-build,network,tls ^
  --target-dir target\dll-release ^
  -- ^
  -Ctarget-feature=+crt-static
```

And Debug:

```bat
cargo rustc ^
  --target i686-rust9x-windows-msvc ^
  --lib ^
  --features dll-build,network,tls ^
  --target-dir target\dll-debug ^
  -- ^
  -Ctarget-feature=+crt-static
```

Rust documents `-C target-feature=+crt-static` as the mechanism for static C runtime linkage. citeturn415868search3turn415868search10

Even better, make the aliases invoke `cargo rustc` instead of `cargo build`, because now the DLL linkage mode is unambiguously part of the build command.

---

## And I would temporarily remove `unicows.lib`

For diagnosis, don't change ten things simultaneously.

Temporarily remove:

```toml
'-Clink-arg=C:\\Program Files\\Microsoft Platform SDK\\Lib\\unicows.lib',
```

from the normal rust9x target flags and rebuild the DLL.

Then try your C# application again.

If:

```text
LoadLibrary = 0
GetLastError = 126
```

changes to successful loading, we've found the dependency.

If you **need** Unicode thunking for the actual Windows 9x target, then we should explicitly package `unicows.dll` with the application rather than accidentally assuming it exists on the host.

## One more thing: the VS2005 `vshost.exe`

Your debugger output says:

```text
HandlerGui.vshost.exe
```

and your actual managed assembly is loaded from:

```text
HandlerGui\bin\Debug
```

This isn't the primary problem because your explicit `LoadLibrary()` receives the full DLL path, so vshost's current directory is irrelevant to that specific load.

However, once the native DLL is fixed, I would also test the actual:

```text
HandlerGui.exe
```

outside the VS2005 debugger.

That removes the vshost layer and gives us a clean test of the actual process environment.

### The important conclusion

The current state is actually useful:

```text
C# managed assembly loads       OK
Rust9xWindowsAuth.dll found    OK
absolute DLL path generated     OK
x86 solution                    OK
LoadLibrary                     FAIL
Win32 error                     126
```

Therefore we should stop touching the C# path logic. **The next target is the native dependency chain.**

The two strongest suspects from the repository are:

1. **`unicows.dll`**, because the Rust target configuration explicitly links `unicows.lib`. fileciteturn36file0
2. **VC80 runtime**, because the intended `crt-static` configuration is currently expressed through invalid Cargo feature-based target cfg matching, while `build.rs` still packages VC80 runtime files. fileciteturn37file0turn415868search1

Run `dumpbin /DEPENDENTS` on the exact DLL from the error dialog. That will let us stop guessing and fix the precise missing dependency.

**ChatGPT** *(12.08.2026, 15:33:14)*

That output nails it. The architecture is fine; the DLL has a **real native dependency problem**.

### What `dumpbin` proves

Your DLL is:

```text
14C machine (x86)
32 bit word machine
```

So the `x86` C# solution is correct.

Its native dependencies are:

```text
secur32.dll
crypt32.dll
kernel32.dll
credui.dll
ws2_32.dll
bcryptprimitives.dll
MSVCR80.dll
```

The important one is:

```text
MSVCR80.dll
```

Your Rust DLL is **dynamically linked to the VC8 CRT**. That directly contradicts the intended `dll-build` configuration, which was supposed to make the DLL CRT-static. The repository's `.cargo/config.toml` attempts to enable `+crt-static` through `cfg(... feature = "dll-build")`; that is not a valid way to select Cargo feature-dependent target configuration. The crate's `dll-release` / `dll-debug` aliases do pass `--features dll-build`, but that doesn't activate those `target` config sections. fileciteturn36file0turn34file0

So the actual dependency chain is currently:

```text
HandlerGui.exe
    |
    +-- rust9x_windows_auth.dll
            |
            +-- MSVCR80.dll        <-- actual problem
            +-- secur32.dll
            +-- crypt32.dll
            +-- kernel32.dll
            +-- credui.dll
            +-- ws2_32.dll
            +-- bcryptprimitives.dll
```

The `126` you're getting is therefore completely consistent with the native loader failing to satisfy `MSVCR80.dll` or its VC8 side-by-side activation requirements.

## There are two ways to fix this

### Option A — actually build the Rust DLL with static CRT

This is what I would prefer for your project.

The DLL then becomes:

```text
rust9x_windows_auth.dll
    |
    +-- secur32.dll
    +-- crypt32.dll
    +-- kernel32.dll
    +-- credui.dll
    +-- ws2_32.dll
    +-- bcryptprimitives.dll
```

and **no `MSVCR80.dll` dependency**.

The cleanest way is to put the CRT choice directly into the Cargo invocation rather than trying to encode it in a `cfg(feature=...)` target section.

For your DLL alias, use `cargo rustc`:

```toml
[alias]
dll-release = [
    "rustc",
    "--target",
    "i686-rust9x-windows-msvc",
    "--release",
    "--lib",
    "--features",
    "dll-build,network,tls",
    "--target-dir",
    "target/dll-release",
    "--",
    "-Ctarget-feature=+crt-static"
]

dll-debug = [
    "rustc",
    "--target",
    "i686-rust9x-windows-msvc",
    "--lib",
    "--features",
    "dll-build,network,tls",
    "--target-dir",
    "target/dll-debug",
    "--",
    "-Ctarget-feature=+crt-static"
]
```

Then rebuild from scratch:

```bat
cargo clean
cargo +rust9x dll-debug
```

and check again:

```bat
dumpbin /DEPENDENTS "...\rust9x_windows_auth.dll"
```

The key test is that `MSVCR80.dll` disappears.

### Option B — package VC80 CRT correctly

Your current `build.rs` already tries to do exactly that: it searches WinSxS, copies `msvcr80.dll`, `msvcp80.dll`, `msvcm80.dll`, and generates/copies `Microsoft.VC80.CRT.manifest`. fileciteturn37file0

But there's an important detail: **having**

```text
HandlerGui\bin\Debug\MSVCR80.dll
```

by itself does not necessarily satisfy a VC8 assembly dependency. The VC8 CRT commonly participates in Windows side-by-side activation, which is why your project is also generating:

```text
Microsoft.VC80.CRT.manifest
```

and putting the runtime under:

```text
Microsoft.VC80.CRT\
```

So if you take this route, the final application output needs the complete VC80 assembly layout, and the assembly identity in the manifest has to match the DLLs you copied.

---

# But there's another dependency worth noticing

This:

```text
bcryptprimitives.dll
```

is a **modern Windows API dependency**.

That isn't going to exist on the old Windows versions you're ultimately targeting. So even after fixing `MSVCR80.dll`, the current DLL is not yet actually a Windows 2000/9x-compatible DLL.

Likewise:

```text
credui.dll
```

is an important compatibility boundary.

This is separate from today's C# loading failure, because your current system gets as far as running the x86 .NET 2.0 application. But it matters for the actual intended deployment.

---

# Right now, don't touch `CentralFile.cs`

Your C# loader is doing exactly what it should:

```text
Find DLL
    ↓
absolute path
    ↓
LoadLibrary
    ↓
126
```

The error is coming from Windows' native dependency resolver **after it successfully locates the DLL**.

Your screenshot + debugger output + `dumpbin` establish that very cleanly.

I'd leave:

```text
FindDllPath()
LoadLibrary()
GetProcAddress()
```

alone for the moment.

---

# I would also add one excellent diagnostic to `CentralFile.cs`

When `LoadLibrary()` returns 126, enumerate the expected runtime files beside the DLL and explicitly report whether they're there.

For example:

```csharp
private static void LogNativeDependencies(string dllPath)
{
    string dir = Path.GetDirectoryName(dllPath);

    string[] files = new string[]
    {
        Path.Combine(dir, "MSVCR80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "MSVCP80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "MSVCM80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT", "MSVCR80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT", "MSVCP80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT", "MSVCM80.dll"),
        Path.Combine(dir, "Microsoft.VC80.CRT", "Microsoft.VC80.CRT.manifest")
    };

    Trace.WriteLine("=== VC80 dependency diagnostic ===");

    for (int i = 0; i < files.Length; i++)
    {
        Trace.WriteLine(
            File.Exists(files[i])
                ? "FOUND: " + files[i]
                : "MISSING: " + files[i]);
    }
}
```

Call it immediately before `LoadLibrary()`.

That gives us a much more useful log:

```text
=== VC80 dependency diagnostic ===
FOUND: E:\...\MSVCR80.dll
FOUND: E:\...\Microsoft.VC80.CRT\MSVCR80.dll
FOUND: E:\...\Microsoft.VC80.CRT\MSVCP80.dll
FOUND: E:\...\Microsoft.VC80.CRT\MSVCM80.dll
FOUND: E:\...\Microsoft.VC80.CRT\Microsoft.VC80.CRT.manifest
```

or immediately exposes what's missing.

## And the definitive diagnostic for error 126

On the machine where you're testing, run:

```bat
sxstrace trace -logfile:E:\temp\rust9x_sxs.etl
```

then launch `HandlerGui`, let it hit the `126`, and stop tracing:

```bat
sxstrace stoptrace
```

Convert it:

```bat
sxstrace parse E:\temp\rust9x_sxs.etl -outfile:E:\temp\rust9x_sxs.txt
```

That will tell us whether the VC80 assembly activation is failing because of the manifest, version, architecture, or missing CRT component.

### My recommendation

Fix the Rust build first so:

```text
dumpbin /DEPENDENTS rust9x_windows_auth.dll
```

**no longer lists `MSVCR80.dll`**.

Then the current `LoadLibrary()` failure should disappear without needing increasingly elaborate C# search-path hacks.

After that, we can deal with the remaining legacy-Windows dependencies such as `bcryptprimitives.dll` and determine which of them are coming from `windows-sys`/`sspi` versus the Windows 9x compatibility layer.
